Import icon you wish to use from one of the iconsets.

```ts
import { AchievementIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato';
import { SmileyIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-emoticon';
import { MyAviatorIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-illustration';
import { FolderIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-mime';
import { ErrorIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import { AddIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/micro-focus';
import { ActionIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/ux-aspects';
```

## API Reference

### Icon Component

The icon components can be used to display an icon from UX Aspects Universal Iconsets.
Icons can also be mirrored for use in RTL layouts where applicable.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `size` | `string` | - | No | Define the size of the icon. |
| `mirror` | `boolean` | - | No | Whether the icon should be mirrored. |
| `mirrorInRtl` | `boolean` | - | No | Whether the icon should be mirrored automatically in RTL mode. |
| `label` | `string` | - | No | Define the label for accessibility. |
| `role` | `string` | - | No | Define the SVG role attribute for accessibility. |

## Icon List

---

Infotips provide additional, inline information about a screen element within its context of use. More robust than Tooltips, if a command is seldom used or complex, an Infotip can provide more information than a tooltip. As per our convention Infotips open on click, whereas tooltips open on hover or focus. If the information should contain additional interactive elements such as links or form elements, use a Flyout panel instead.

[UXD guidelines](https://uxd.opentext.net/guidelines/info_tip.htm)

## Import

Import `Infotip` to use the component.

```ts
import { Infotip } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/popover';
```

## API Reference

The following exports are available within `@ux-aspects-universal/react/popover`:

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `isOpen` | `boolean` | - | No | Determine if the Infotip is open or closed. |
| `placement` | `above | below | before | after` | `above` | No | Where the Infotip is positioned vertically relative to the trigger. |
| `variant` | `auto | light | dark` | `auto` | No | The color variant of the Infotip. |
| `triggerRef` | `RefObject` | - | Yes | The element used to trigger Infotip. The Infotip will be positioned relative to this element. |
| `openTriggers` | `click | mouseenter | focus` | `['click']` | No | Events that will cause the Infotip to appear. |
| `closeTriggers` | `click | mouseleave | blur | escape | clickoutside | mouseoutside` | `['clickoutside', 'blur']` | No | Events that will cause the Infotip to hide. |
| `onOpenChange` | `(isOpen: boolean) => void` | - | No | Emit whenever the Infotip is opened or closed. |
| `isDisabled` | `boolean` | - | No | Determine if the Infotip is disabled. |
| `offset` | `number` | - | No | The offset of the Infotip from the trigger element. |
| `delay` | `number` | - | No | The delay before the Infotip is shown in milliseconds. |
| `minWidth` | `number` | `160` | No | The minimum width of the Infotip. |
| `container` | `HTMLElement` | - | No | The container element the infotip will be appended to. This defaults to the document body. |

## Examples

### Placement

The Infotip component supports four placement options: above, below, before, and after.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { Infotip, Placement, useInfotipTrigger } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/popover';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const InfotipButton: FC<{ label: string; placement: Placement }> = ({ label, placement }) => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);
  const { infotipProps, triggerProps } = useInfotipTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>({
    isOpen,
    onOpenChange: setIsOpen,
  });

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary {...triggerProps}>
        {label}
      </Button>
      <Infotip {...infotipProps} placement={placement}>
        <span>Infotip {placement}</span>
      </Infotip>
    </>
  );
};

const InfotipPlacementExample: FC = () => {
  return (
    <div
      style={{
        display: 'flex',
        flexDirection: 'column',
        alignItems: 'center',
        gap: 40,
      }}
    >
      <InfotipButton label="Above" placement="above" />
      <div style={{ display: 'flex', gap: '150px' }}>
        <InfotipButton label="Before" placement="before" />
        <InfotipButton label="After" placement="after" />
      </div>
      <InfotipButton label="Below" placement="below" />
    </div>
  );
};

export default InfotipPlacementExample;
```

---

This is used for sub-content within a content container or modal in order to separate it from the main content.

```tsx
import { IconButton } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { CloseSmallIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato';
import {
  InlineContentPanel,
  InlineContentPanelHeader,
  InlineContentPanelContent,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-content-panel';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

export const InlineContentPanelExample: FC = () => (
  <InlineContentPanel style={{ maxWidth: '224px' }}>
    <InlineContentPanelHeader
      actionButtons={
        <IconButton
          key="close"
          id="close-btn"
          aria-label="Close"
          flat
          secondary
          size="small"
          onClick={() => console.log('Close button clicked')}
          icon={<CloseSmallIcon />}
        />
      }
    >
      Panel title
    </InlineContentPanelHeader>
    <InlineContentPanelContent>
      The inline content panel is used within a content container or modal in order to separate
      sub-content from the main content. This panel can be used with different types of content such
      as faceted filters or sidebar navigation. The panel title can vary in length and will wrap
      onto multiple lines rather than truncating. If the panel appears within a container from where
      it is triggered by a toggle within the header, the panel will emerge directly below the header
      with no space between them. The inline content panel can be used in conjunction with the
      Splitter component if a variable width panel is required.
    </InlineContentPanelContent>
  </InlineContentPanel>
);

export default InlineContentPanelExample;
```

## Import

Import `InlineContentPanel` to use the component.

```ts
import {
  InlineContentPanel,
  InlineContentPanelHeader,
  InlineContentPanelContent,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-content-panel';
```

## API Reference

The following exports are available within `@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-content-panel`:

### InlineContentPanel

The wrapping container of the inline content panel.

### InlineContentPanelHeader

The header of the inline content panel. It consists of a panel title and optional action buttons.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `actionButtons` | `ReactNode` | - | No | Optionally defines custom action buttons. |

### InlineContentPanelContent

The content of the inline content panel.

## Examples

The following example demonstrates how to use the Inline Content Panel component.

### Inline Content Panel with Splitter

```tsx
import { faker } from '@faker-js/faker';
import { IconButton } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { ChevronDoubleDownIcon, DocumentIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato';
import { FolderIcon, PdfIcon, PodcastIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-mime';
import {
  InlineContentPanel,
  InlineContentPanelContent,
  InlineContentPanelHeader,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-content-panel';
import { useSelection } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/selection';
import { Splitter, SplitterArea } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/splitter';
import {
  Table,
  TableBody,
  TableCell,
  TableHead,
  TableHeaderCell,
  TableRow,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/table';
import { Tree, TreeNodeModel, useTreeControl } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/tree';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useMemo } from 'react';

const resources: Resource[] = Array.from({ length: 16 }).map(() => ({
  name:
    faker.system.fileName({ extensionCount: 0 }) + faker.helpers.arrayElement(['.pdf', '.mp3', '']),
  size:
    faker.number.int({ min: 1, max: 600 }) + ' ' + faker.helpers.arrayElement(['KB', 'MB', 'GB']),
  owner: faker.person.fullName(),
  status: faker.datatype.boolean(),
}));

const getIcon = (name: string): React.ReactNode => {
  switch (name.split('.').pop()) {
    case 'pdf':
      return <PdfIcon size="24px" />;
    case 'mp3':
      return <PodcastIcon size="24px" />;
    default:
      return <FolderIcon size="24px" />;
  }
};

export const InlineContentPanelSplitterExample: FC = () => {
  const items = useMemo<TreeNodeModel[]>(
    () => [
      {
        title: 'Content type',
        children: [
          {
            title: 'Agreement',
          },
          {
            title: 'Analysis',
          },
          {
            title: 'Instructions',
          },
          {
            title: 'Proposals',
          },
        ],
      },
      {
        title: 'File type',
        children: [
          {
            title: 'Audio',
          },
          {
            title: 'CAD',
          },
          {
            title: 'Database',
          },
          {
            title: 'Image',
          },
          {
            title: 'Value',
          },
        ],
      },
    ],
    []
  );

  const treeControl = useTreeControl({ items });
  const selectionManager = useSelection({ mode: 'single' });

  return (
    <Splitter>
      <SplitterArea defaultSize={224} minSize={200} maxSize={400}>
        <InlineContentPanel>
          <InlineContentPanelHeader
            actionButtons={
              <IconButton
                key="expand"
                id="expand-btn"
                aria-label="Expand"
                flat
                secondary
                size="small"
                icon={<ChevronDoubleDownIcon />}
              />
            }
          >
            Refine by
          </InlineContentPanelHeader>
          <InlineContentPanelContent>
            <Tree treeControl={treeControl} selectionManager={selectionManager} />
          </InlineContentPanelContent>
        </InlineContentPanel>
      </SplitterArea>
      <SplitterArea>
        <Table style={{ width: '100%' }}>
          <TableHead>
            <TableRow>
              <TableHeaderCell align="center" style={{ width: 40, lineHeight: '8px' }}>
                <DocumentIcon size="16px" />
              </TableHeaderCell>

              <TableHeaderCell>Name</TableHeaderCell>
              <TableHeaderCell>Owned by</TableHeaderCell>
            </TableRow>
          </TableHead>
          <TableBody>
            {resources.map(resource => (
              <TableRow key={resource.name} tabIndex={0}>
                <TableCell style={{ lineHeight: '8px' }}>{getIcon(resource.name)}</TableCell>

                <TableCell>{resource.name}</TableCell>
                <TableCell>{resource.owner}</TableCell>
              </TableRow>
            ))}
          </TableBody>
        </Table>
      </SplitterArea>
    </Splitter>
  );
};

export default InlineContentPanelSplitterExample;

interface Resource {
  name: string;
  owner: string;
}
```

---

This is a wrapper for form input components to allow their state to be toggled from a read-only mode for viewing to an edit mode for changing content.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { TextInput } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/text-input';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const TextInputInlineEditExample: FC = () => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('John Smith');

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
      <FormFieldLabel>Full name</FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit value={value} onCancel={setValue}>
        <TextInput
          type="text"
          placeholder="Enter first name last name"
          value={value}
          onValueChange={setValue}
        />
      </InlineEdit>
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default TextInputInlineEditExample;
```

## Import

Import `InlineEdit` to use the component.

```ts
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
```

## API Reference

The following exports are available within `@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit`:

### InlineEdit

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `value` | `T` | - | No | The current value of the underlying control. |
| `placeholder` | `string` | - | No | The placeholder text to display when no value is present. |
| `size` | `'small' | 'medium' | 'large'` | - | No | The size of the control. |
| `isEditing` | `boolean` | - | No | Whether the control is currently in an editable state. |
| `canSave` | `boolean` | - | No | Whether the control can be saved. This can be used to disable the save button when implementing custom validation. |
| `canReset` | `boolean` | - | No | Whether the control can be reset. |
| `formatter` | `(value: T) => string | string[]` | - | No | Format the value for display. This is required if the value is not a string or string array. |
| `onEditingChange` | `(isEditing: boolean) => void` | - | No | Emit when the editing state changes. |
| `onReset` | `() => void` | - | No | Emit when the reset button is clicked. |
| `onSave` | `(value: T) => void` | - | No | Emit when the save button is clicked. |
| `onCancel` | `(value: T) => void` | - | No | Emit when the cancel button is clicked. This is called with the value before editing started. This can be used to revert the value back to its original state. |
| `editButtonAriaLabel` | `string` | - | No | The aria label for the edit button. |
| `resetButtonAriaLabel` | `string` | - | No | The aria label for the reset button. |
| `saveButtonAriaLabel` | `string` | - | No | The aria label for the save button. |
| `cancelButtonAriaLabel` | `string` | - | No | The aria label for the cancel button. |
| `saveButton` | `() => ReactNode` | - | No | Customize the save button. |
| `cancelButton` | `() => ReactNode` | - | No | Customize the cancel button. |
| `resetButton` | `() => ReactNode` | - | No | Customize the reset button. |
| `editButton` | `() => ReactNode` | - | No | Customize the edit button. |

### InlineEditSaveButton

A preconfigured icon button that can be used with the `saveButton` render prop.

### InlineEditCancelButton

A preconfigured icon button that can be used with the `cancelButton` render prop.

### InlineEditResetButton

A preconfigured icon button that can be used with the `resetButton` render prop.

### InlineEditBeginEditButton

A preconfigured icon button that can be used with the `editButton` render prop.

## Examples

The following examples demonstrate how to use the Inline Edit component in different scenarios.

### Inline edit with a text area

A text area can be used as the underlying control for the inline edit component.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { TextArea } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/text-input';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const TextAreaInlineEditExample: FC = () => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('');

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
      <FormFieldLabel>Comments</FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit value={value} onCancel={setValue}>
        <TextArea
          placeholder="Enter comments"
          value={value}
          onValueChange={setValue}
          style={{ maxWidth: 220, maxHeight: 140 }}
        />
      </InlineEdit>
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default TextAreaInlineEditExample;
```

### Inline edit with checkboxes

Checkboxes can be used as the underlying control for the inline edit component.

```tsx
import { Checkbox, CheckboxGroup } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/checkbox';
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const CheckboxInlineEditExample: FC = () => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState(['Email', 'Mobile']);

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
      <FormFieldLabel>Contact preferences</FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit value={value} onCancel={setValue}>
        <CheckboxGroup value={value} onValueChange={setValue}>
          <Checkbox value="Email">Email</Checkbox>
          <Checkbox value="Mail">Mail</Checkbox>
          <Checkbox value="Mobile">Mobile</Checkbox>
          <Checkbox value="SMS">SMS</Checkbox>
        </CheckboxGroup>
      </InlineEdit>
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default CheckboxInlineEditExample;
```

### Inline edit with radio buttons

Radio buttons can be used as the underlying control for the inline edit component.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { RadioButton, RadioGroup } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/radio-button';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const RadioButtonInlineEditExample: FC = () => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('Strongly Agree');

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
      <FormFieldLabel>Survey response</FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit value={value} onCancel={setValue}>
        <RadioGroup value={value} onValueChange={setValue}>
          <RadioButton value="Strongly Agree">Strongly Agree</RadioButton>
          <RadioButton value="Agree">Agree</RadioButton>
          <RadioButton value="Neutral">Neutral</RadioButton>
          <RadioButton value="Disagree">Disagree</RadioButton>
          <RadioButton value="Strongly Disagree">Strongly Disagree</RadioButton>
        </RadioGroup>
      </InlineEdit>
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default RadioButtonInlineEditExample;
```

### Inline edit with a select

A select can be used as the underlying control for the inline edit component.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';

import { Select, SelectOption } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/select';

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
      <FormFieldLabel>Assignee</FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit value={value} onCancel={setValue}>
        <Select value={value} onValueChange={setValue}>
          <SelectOption value="John Smith">John Smith</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Jane Doe">Jane Doe</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Alice Johnson">Alice Johnson</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Bob Brown">Bob Brown</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Charlie White">Charlie White</SelectOption>
        </Select>
      </InlineEdit>
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default SelectInlineEditExample;
```

### Inline edit with a combobox

A combobox can be used as the underlying control for the inline edit component.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { Combobox, SelectOption } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/select';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const ComboboxInlineEditExample: FC = () => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState<string | undefined>('John Smith');

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
      <FormFieldLabel>User</FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit value={value} onCancel={setValue}>
        <Combobox<string>
          placeholder="Select or enter user name"
          value={value}
          onValueChange={setValue}
          onFilterChange={setValue}
        >
          <SelectOption value="John Smith">John Smith</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Jane Doe">Jane Doe</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Alice Johnson">Alice Johnson</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Bob Brown">Bob Brown</SelectOption>
          <SelectOption value="Charlie White">Charlie White</SelectOption>
        </Combobox>
      </InlineEdit>
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default ComboboxInlineEditExample;
```

### Inline edit with validation

Validation can be implemented to prevent saving invalid values e.g. saving an empty value for a mandatory field. Validation can be implemented using the `canSave` prop.

```tsx
import {
  FormField,
  FormFieldFeedback,
  FormFieldLabel,
  RequiredIndicator,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { StatusErrorFilledIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/micro-focus';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { TextInput } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/text-input';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const InlineEditValidationExample: FC = () => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('Cannot add a new workspace.');
  const [isEditing, setIsEditing] = useState(false);

  // this could be handled by a form library like Formik or React Hook Form
  const isValid = value.trim().length > 0;

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }} isValid={isValid}>
      <FormFieldLabel>
        <RequiredIndicator />
        Issue reported
      </FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit
        value={value}
        canSave={isValid}
        onCancel={setValue}
        isEditing={isEditing}
        onEditingChange={setIsEditing}
      >
        <TextInput
          type="text"
          placeholder="Enter full name"
          value={value}
          onValueChange={setValue}
        />
      </InlineEdit>

      {!isValid && isEditing && (
        <FormFieldFeedback status="error" icon={<StatusErrorFilledIcon />}>
          Issue reported is required
        </FormFieldFeedback>
      )}
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default InlineEditValidationExample;
```

### Inline edit with reset

The reset button can be used to revert the value back to its original state. This can be enabled by setting the `canReset` property to `true`.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { TextInput } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/text-input';
import { useFormik } from 'formik';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const ResetInlineEditExample: FC = () => {
  // any form library can be used here, or useState can be used either
  const formik = useFormik({
    initialValues: { fullName: 'John Smith' },
    onSubmit: () => {
      // handle form submission
    },
  });

  return (
    <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
      <FormFieldLabel>Full name</FormFieldLabel>

      <InlineEdit
        value={formik.values['fullName']}
        onCancel={() => formik.resetForm()}
        canReset={formik.dirty}
        onReset={formik.resetForm}
      >
        <TextInput
          type="text"
          name="fullName"
          placeholder="Enter first name last name"
          value={formik.values['fullName']}
          onChange={formik.handleChange}
          onBlur={formik.handleBlur}
        />
      </InlineEdit>
    </FormField>
  );
};

export default ResetInlineEditExample;
```

### Inline edit with size variations

The size of the control can be adjusted using the `size` property.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { TextInput } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/text-input';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const InlineEditSizesExample: FC = () => {
  const [emailAddress, setEmailAddress] = useState('johnsmith@email.com');

  return (
    <div style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '1rem', alignItems: 'center' }}>
      <FormField style={{ width: 240 }}>
        <FormFieldLabel>Email address</FormFieldLabel>

        <InlineEdit value={emailAddress} onCancel={setEmailAddress} size="small">
          <TextInput
            type="text"
            placeholder="Enter email address"
            value={emailAddress}
            onValueChange={setEmailAddress}
            size="small"
          />
        </InlineEdit>
      </FormField>

      <FormField style={{ width: 240 }}>
        <FormFieldLabel>Email address</FormFieldLabel>

        <InlineEdit value={emailAddress} onCancel={setEmailAddress} size="medium">
          <TextInput
            type="text"
            placeholder="Enter email address"
            value={emailAddress}
            onValueChange={setEmailAddress}
            size="medium"
          />
        </InlineEdit>
      </FormField>

      <FormField style={{ width: 240 }}>
        <FormFieldLabel>Email address</FormFieldLabel>

        <InlineEdit value={emailAddress} onCancel={setEmailAddress} size="large">
          <TextInput
            type="email"
            placeholder="Enter email address"
            value={emailAddress}
            onValueChange={setEmailAddress}
            size="large"
          />
        </InlineEdit>
      </FormField>
    </div>
  );
};

export default InlineEditSizesExample;
```

### Inline edit with tooltips

Inline edit buttons can be customized using the `editButton`, `saveButton`, `cancelButton` and `resetButton` render props.
An example of this might be to add a tooltip to a button.

```tsx
import { FormField, FormFieldLabel } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/form';
import { InlineEdit, InlineEditBeginEditingButton } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-edit';
import { Tooltip, useTooltipTrigger } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/popover';
import { TextInput } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/text-input';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const InlineEditTooltipExample: FC = () => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('John Smith');

  const { tooltipProps, triggerProps } = useTooltipTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>();

  return (
    <>
      <FormField style={{ width: 300 }}>
        <FormFieldLabel>Full name</FormFieldLabel>

        <InlineEdit
          value={value}
          onCancel={setValue}
          editButton={() => <InlineEditBeginEditingButton {...triggerProps} />}
        >
          <TextInput
            type="text"
            placeholder="Enter first name last name"
            value={value}
            onValueChange={setValue}
          />
        </InlineEdit>
      </FormField>
      <Tooltip {...tooltipProps} placement="above">
        Edit your full name
      </Tooltip>
    </>
  );
};

export default InlineEditTooltipExample;
```

## Localization

This component uses several strings which must be localized. You can use `InlineEditI18nContext` to configure these strings.

```tsx
import { InlineEditI18nContext } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/breadcrumbs';

<InlineEditI18nContext.Provider value={{ editButtonAriaLabel: "Modifier", ... }}>
  <InlineEdit />
</InlineEditI18nContext.Provider>
```

The following strings are available for localization:

| Name                      | Description                                |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| **editButtonAriaLabel**   | The aria label for the edit button.        |
| **resetButtonAriaLabel**  | The aria label for the reset button.       |
| **saveButtonAriaLabel**   | The aria label for the save button.        |
| **cancelButtonAriaLabel** | The aria label for the cancel button.      |
| **placeholder**           | The placeholder text when no value is set. |

---

Inline messages are for showing information inline close to an element they are adding context to. For example:

- Errors—Summarizing validation or other issues regarding a field, form or page.
- Information—Showing inline details or messages.
- Warning—Informing the user about important issues regarding the current selections, settings or states.

The message may optionally include a link to further information, an action button and a close icon.

[OT Guidelines on messaging](https://uxd.opentext.net/guidelines/messages.htm)

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { InfoIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import { InlineNotification } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification';
import { Link } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/typography';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const InlineNotificationExample: FC = () => (
  <InlineNotification
    status="info"
    icon={<InfoIcon />}
    actionButtons={
      <Button secondary flat size="small">
        Button
      </Button>
    }
  >
    Inline message to give feedback to the user. <Link href="#">Link example.</Link>
  </InlineNotification>
);

export default InlineNotificationExample;
```

## Import

Import `InlineNotification` to use the component.

```ts
import { InlineNotification } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification';
```

## API Reference

The following exports are available within `@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification`:

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `status` | `info | success | warning | error` | - | Yes | Define the Inline Message color. |
| `size` | `small | medium | large` | `medium` | - | Define the size of Inline Message. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` | - | - | Optionally define an icon. |
| `isDismissible` | `boolean` | `true` | - | Whether the dismiss Inline Message is visible. |
| `onDismiss` | `() => void` | - | - | Emits when the built-in dismiss Inline Message is clicked. |
| `orientation` | `horizontal | vertical` | `horizontal` | - | Whether the Inline Message is displayed in the stacked variant. |

## Examples

The following examples demonstrate how to use the Inline Message component in different scenarios.

### Status

The Inline Message component supports different status types: `info`, `success`, `warning`, and `error`.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  ErrorIcon,
  InfoIcon,
  SuccessIcon,
  WarningIcon,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import { InlineNotification } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification';
import { Link } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/typography';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const InlineNotificationStatusExample: FC = () => (
  <div
    style={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      gap: '16px',
    }}
  >
    <InlineNotification
      status="info"
      icon={<InfoIcon />}
      actionButtons={
        <Button secondary flat primary size="small">
          Button
        </Button>
      }
    >
      Inline message to give feedback to the user. <Link href="#">Link example.</Link>
    </InlineNotification>
    <InlineNotification
      status="error"
      icon={<ErrorIcon />}
      actionButtons={
        <Button secondary flat primary size="small">
          Button
        </Button>
      }
    >
      Inline message to give feedback to the user. <Link href="#">Link example.</Link>
    </InlineNotification>
    <InlineNotification
      status="warning"
      icon={<WarningIcon />}
      actionButtons={
        <Button secondary flat primary size="small">
          Button
        </Button>
      }
    >
      Inline message to give feedback to the user. <Link href="#">Link example.</Link>
    </InlineNotification>
    <InlineNotification
      status="success"
      icon={<SuccessIcon />}
      actionButtons={
        <Button secondary flat primary size="small">
          Button
        </Button>
      }
    >
      Inline message to give feedback to the user. <Link href="#">Link example.</Link>
    </InlineNotification>
  </div>
);

export default InlineNotificationStatusExample;
```

### Stacked

The Inline Message component can be displayed in the stacked variant.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { InfoIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import { InlineNotification } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification';
import { Link } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/typography';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const InlineNotificationStackedExample: FC = () => (
  <InlineNotification
    orientation="vertical"
    status="info"
    style={{ maxWidth: '400px' }}
    icon={<InfoIcon />}
    actionButtons={
      <Button secondary flat size="small">
        Button
      </Button>
    }
  >
    Inline message on the stacked variant which can be useful for longer messages that may wrap onto
    multiple lines. <Link href="#">Link example.</Link>
  </InlineNotification>
);

export default InlineNotificationStackedExample;
```

### Sizes

The Inline Message component supports three sizes: `small`, `medium`, and `large`.

```tsx
import { InfoIcon, InfoSmallIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import { InlineNotification } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const InlineNotificationSizesExample: FC = () => (
  <div
    style={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      gap: '16px',
    }}
  >
    <InlineNotification isDismissible={false} status="info" size="small" icon={<InfoSmallIcon />}>
      Inline message to give feedback to the user.
    </InlineNotification>

    <InlineNotification isDismissible={false} status="info" size="medium" icon={<InfoIcon />}>
      Inline message to give feedback to the user.
    </InlineNotification>

    <InlineNotification isDismissible={false} status="info" size="large" icon={<InfoIcon />}>
      Inline message to give feedback to the user.
    </InlineNotification>
  </div>
);

export default InlineNotificationSizesExample;
```

### Alignment

The Inline Message centers the content by default, however it can also be aligned to the start.

```tsx
import { InfoIcon, InfoSmallIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import { InlineNotification } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const InlineNotificationAlignmentExample: FC = () => (
  <div
    style={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      gap: '16px',
      width: 400,
    }}
  >
    <InlineNotification
      isDismissible={false}
      status="info"
      size="small"
      alignment="start"
      icon={<InfoSmallIcon />}
    >
      Inline message to give feedback to the user.
    </InlineNotification>

    <InlineNotification
      isDismissible={false}
      status="info"
      size="medium"
      alignment="start"
      icon={<InfoIcon />}
    >
      Inline message to give feedback to the user.
    </InlineNotification>

    <InlineNotification
      isDismissible={false}
      status="info"
      size="large"
      alignment="start"
      icon={<InfoIcon />}
    >
      Inline message to give feedback to the user.
    </InlineNotification>
  </div>
);

export default InlineNotificationAlignmentExample;
```

## Localization

This component uses strings which must be localized. You can use `InlineNotificationI18nContext` function to configure these strings.

```tsx
import { InlineNotificationI18nContext } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/inline-notification';

<InlineNotificationI18nContext.Provider value={{ dismissButtonAriaLabel: "Le dismiss button", ... }}>
  <InlineNotification />
</InlineNotificationI18nContext.Provider>
```

The following strings are available for localization:

| Name                       | Description                            | Type     |
| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------- | -------- |
| **dismissButtonAriaLabel** | The aria label for the dismiss button. | `string` |

---

A menu provides a list of options or actions.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { Menu, MenuItem, useMenuTrigger } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const MenuExample: FC = () => {
  const { triggerProps, menuProps } = useMenuTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>();

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary type="button" {...triggerProps}>
        Actions
      </Button>
      <Menu {...menuProps}>
        <MenuItem>Option 1</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem>Option 2</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem>Option 3</MenuItem>
      </Menu>
    </>
  );
};

export default MenuExample;
```

## Import

Import the `Menu` to use the component.

```ts
import {
  Menu,
  MenuItem,
  MenuGroup,
  MenuGroupHeader,
  useMenuTrigger,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu';
```

## API Reference

The following exports are available within `@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu`:

### useMenuTrigger

The `useMenuTrigger` hook provides can be used to hook up the menu with its trigger element and control the open state.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `isOpen` | `boolean` | - | - | The open state of the menu. |
| `onOpenChange` | `(isOpen: boolean) => void` | - | - | Callback to handle the open state change. |
| `anchorRef` | `React.RefObject` | - | - | The element the menu is anchored to. |
| `triggerRef` | `React.RefObject` | - | - | The trigger element that opens the menu. |
| `triggerId` | `string` | - | - | Define a unique id for the trigger element. |
| `menuId` | `string` | - | - | Define a unique id for the menu element. |

The hook returns an object with the following properties:

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `isOpen` | `boolean` | - | - | The open state of the menu. |
| `setIsOpen` | `(isOpen: boolean) => void` | - | - | Set the open state of the menu. |
| `triggerProps` | `TriggerProps` | - | - | Props to spread on the trigger element. |
| `menuProps` | `MenuProps` | - | - | Props to spread on the menu element. |

### Menu

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `isOpen` | `boolean` | - | - | Whether the menu is open. |
| `placement` | `'above' | 'below'` | - | No | Where the menu is positioned vertically relative to the trigger. |
| `alignment` | `'start' | 'end'` | - | No | Where the menu is positioned horizontally relative to the trigger. |
| `triggerRef` | `ReactNode` | - | - | The trigger element for the menu. |
| `onClose` | `() => void` | - | - | Emits when the menu closes. |
| `closeOnBlur` | `boolean` | - | - | Whether the menu should close when the user tabs out of the menu. |

### MenuItem

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `isDisabled` | `boolean` | - | - | Whether the menu item is disabled. |
| `subMenu` | `ReactNode` | - | - | Define the submenu. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` | - | - | Define the leading icon. |

### MenuGroup

Used to define a group of menu items.

### MenuGroupHeader

Used to define a header for a group of menu items.

### MenuItem

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | - | No | Whether the menu item is disabled. |

### MenuToggleItem

A menu item that can be toggled.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `isChecked` | `boolean` | - | No | The state of the toggle. |
| `isDisabled` | `boolean` | - | No | Whether the menu item is disabled. |
| `onCheckedChange` | `(isChecked: boolean) => void` | - | No | Emits when the user changes the state of the toggle. |

### MenuDivider

Visually separate menu items.

## Examples

### Sub menu

The Menu component can contain sub menus.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { Menu, MenuItem, useMenuTrigger } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const MenuSubmenuExample: FC = () => {
  const { triggerProps, menuProps } = useMenuTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>();

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary type="button" {...triggerProps}>
        Actions
      </Button>
      <Menu {...menuProps}>
        <MenuItem
          subMenu={
            <>
              <MenuItem>Sub option 1</MenuItem>
              <MenuItem>Sub option 2</MenuItem>
            </>
          }
        >
          Sub-menu
        </MenuItem>
        <MenuItem>Option 1</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem>Option 2</MenuItem>
      </Menu>
    </>
  );
};

export default MenuSubmenuExample;
```

### Groups

The Menu component can contain groups of menu items.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Menu,
  MenuGroup,
  MenuGroupHeader,
  MenuItem,
  useMenuTrigger,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const MenuGroupExample: FC = () => {
  const { triggerProps, menuProps } = useMenuTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>();

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary type="button" {...triggerProps}>
        Actions
      </Button>
      <Menu {...menuProps}>
        <MenuGroup>
          <MenuGroupHeader>Header 1</MenuGroupHeader>
          <MenuItem>Option 1</MenuItem>
          <MenuItem>Option 2</MenuItem>
        </MenuGroup>
        <MenuGroup>
          <MenuGroupHeader>Header 2</MenuGroupHeader>
          <MenuItem>Option 1</MenuItem>
          <MenuItem>Option 2</MenuItem>
        </MenuGroup>
      </Menu>
    </>
  );
};

export default MenuGroupExample;
```

### Checkbox

A Menu with items that can be toggled.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { Menu, MenuToggleItem, useMenuTrigger } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const MenuCheckboxExample: FC = () => {
  const { triggerProps, menuProps } = useMenuTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>();
  const [option1Checked, setOption1Checked] = useState(false);
  const [option2Checked, setOption2Checked] = useState(false);
  const [option3Checked, setOption3Checked] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary type="button" {...triggerProps}>
        Actions
      </Button>
      <Menu {...menuProps}>
        <MenuToggleItem isChecked={option1Checked} onCheckedChange={setOption1Checked}>
          Option 1
        </MenuToggleItem>
        <MenuToggleItem isChecked={option2Checked} onCheckedChange={setOption2Checked}>
          Option 2
        </MenuToggleItem>
        <MenuToggleItem isChecked={option3Checked} onCheckedChange={setOption3Checked}>
          Option 3
        </MenuToggleItem>
      </Menu>
    </>
  );
};

export default MenuCheckboxExample;
```

```tsx
          <MenuItem>Classic appearance</MenuItem>
        </MenuGroup>
        <MenuDivider />
```


### User Menu

The Menu component can be customised to display user information.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { Menu, MenuItem, useMenuTrigger } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC } from 'react';

const MenuUserRowExample: FC = () => {
  const { triggerProps, menuProps } = useMenuTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>();

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary type="button" {...triggerProps}>
        Actions
      </Button>
      <Menu {...menuProps}>
        {users.map((user, i) => (
          <MenuItem key={i} style={rowStyles['menuItem']}>
            <div style={rowStyles['userContainer']}>
              <img
                style={rowStyles['userThumbnail']}
                src="./assets/card-thumbnail.svg"
                alt="card thumbnail"
              />

              <div style={rowStyles['userInfo']}>
                <div>
                  <p style={rowStyles['userName']}>{user.name}</p>
                  <p style={rowStyles['userEmail']}>{user.email}</p>
                </div>

                <div>
                  <p style={rowStyles['userRole']}>{user.role}</p>
                  <p style={rowStyles['userOrg']}>{user.organization}</p>
                </div>

                <span style={rowStyles['userLocation']}>{user.location}</span>
              </div>
            </div>
          </MenuItem>
        ))}
      </Menu>
    </>
  );
};

interface User {
  name: string;
  email: string;
  role: string;
  organization: string;
  location: string;
}

const users: User[] = [
  {
    name: 'Rory Bean',
    email: 'roybean@xyz.com',
    role: 'Associate Document Imaging Administrator',
    organization: 'OT-Portfolio Group',
    location: 'San Antonio',
  },
  {
    name: 'Maya Johnson',
    email: 'mayaj@xyz.com',
    role: 'Senior Systems Analyst',
    organization: 'Data Solutions LLC',
    location: 'Austin',
  },
  {
    name: 'Liam Nguyen',
    email: 'liamn@xyz.com',
    role: 'Network Security Specialist',
    organization: 'TechGuard Inc.',
    location: 'Houston',
  },
];

const rowStyles: Record<string, React.CSSProperties> = {
  menuItem: {
    '--uxa-menu-item__padding-inline': '8px 16px',
    '--uxa-menu-item__padding-block': '8px',
  } as React.CSSProperties,
  userContainer: {
    display: 'flex',
    gap: '8px',
  },
  userThumbnail: {
    width: '32px',
    height: '32px',
  },
  userInfo: {
    display: 'flex',
    alignItems: 'flex-start',
    gap: '8px',
    margin: '0',
    whiteSpace: 'normal',
    fontWeight: 400,
    flexDirection: 'column',
    textAlign: 'left',
  },
  userName: {
    color: 'var(--jato-cta-content-color-tertiary)',
    fontSize: '13px',
    marginTop: '0',
    marginBottom: '1px',
  },
  userEmail: {
    color: 'var(--jato-typography-text-color-tertiary)',
    fontWeight: 500,
    fontSize: '11px',
    margin: '0',
  },
  userRole: {
    color: 'var(--jato-typography-text-color-secondary)',
    fontSize: '12px',
    marginBottom: '4px',
    margin: '0',
  },
  userOrg: {
    color: 'var(--jato-typography-text-color-secondary)',
    fontSize: '12px',
    margin: '0',
  },
  userLocation: {
    color: 'var(--jato-typography-text-color-secondary)',
    fontSize: '12px',
    margin: '0',
  },
};

export default MenuUserRowExample;
```

### Dynamic Loader

The Menu can be configured to load items dynamically. Here is an example of how to use the loader for async data.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import { ServerErrorXsmallIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-illustration';
import { Loader } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/loader';
import { Menu, useMenuTrigger } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/menu';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useEffect, useState } from 'react';

const MenuAsyncExample: FC = () => {
  const { triggerProps, menuProps } = useMenuTrigger<HTMLButtonElement>();
  const [type, setType] = useState<MenuVariant>('default');

  useEffect(() => {
    const intervalId = setInterval(() => {
      setType(prevType => {
        if (prevType === 'default') {
          return 'longer';
        } else if (prevType === 'longer') {
          return 'fail';
        } else {
          return 'default';
        }
      });
    }, 3000);

    return () => clearInterval(intervalId);
  }, []);

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary type="button" {...triggerProps}>
        Actions
      </Button>
      <Menu {...menuProps}>
        <DynamicLoader variant={type} />
      </Menu>
    </>
  );
};

interface DynamicLoaderProps {
  variant: MenuVariant;
}

const DynamicLoader: FC<DynamicLoaderProps> = ({ variant }) => {
  return (
    <div style={styles['container']}>
      <div style={styles['loaderContainer']}>
        {(variant === 'default' || variant === 'longer') && <Loader style={styles['loader']} />}
        {variant === 'fail' && <ServerErrorXsmallIcon size="48px" />}
      </div>
      <div style={styles['footerContainer']}>
        {variant === 'longer' && <span style={styles['text']}>This might take a moment...</span>}
        {variant === 'fail' && (
          <>
            <p style={styles['text']}>Unable to load the content.</p>
            <Button size="small">Try again</Button>
          </>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
};

const styles: Record<string, React.CSSProperties> = {
  container: {
    display: 'flex',
    flexDirection: 'column',
    alignItems: 'center',
    justifyContent: 'center',
    height: 158,
    width: 267,
    rowGap: 8,
    paddingInline: 16,
    paddingBlock: 24,
    boxSizing: 'border-box',
  },
  loaderContainer: {
    display: 'flex',
    alignItems: 'center',
    justifyContent: 'center',
    height: 48,
    width: 48,
  },
  footerContainer: {
    display: 'flex',
    flexDirection: 'column',
    alignItems: 'center',
    rowGap: 12,
    height: 54,
  },
  text: {
    margin: 0,
    fontWeight: 350,
    fontSize: 13,
    lineHeight: '18px',
    color: 'var(--jato-typography-text-color-secondary)',
  },
};

type MenuVariant = 'default' | 'longer' | 'fail';

export default MenuAsyncExample;
```

---

Modal messages are used to convey critical information to the users. They stop the flow of the user, focusing them on one task or piece of information before returning to the flow.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
import { SuccessIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const SuccessModalExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal message
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog icon={<SuccessIcon />} status="success" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader>Success Modal</DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>This is the message content area.</DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter>
            <Button primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Close
            </Button>
          </DialogFooter>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default SuccessModalExample;
```

## Import

Import `Dialog` to use the component.

```ts
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
  DialogDismissButton,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
```

## API Reference

The following exports are available within `@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog`:

### Dialog

The `Dialog` component is used to display a modal dialog.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `size` | `'small' | 'medium' | 'large'` | `medium` | - | The size of the modal. |
| `closeOnBackdropClick` | `boolean` | `true` | - | Whether the modal should close when the backdrop is clicked. |
| `closeOnEscape` | `boolean` | `true` | - | Whether the modal should close when the escape key is pressed. |
| `onClose` | `() => void` | - | - | Function to be called when the modal is closed. |
| `width` | `string` | - | - | The width of the modal in CSS units. |
| `height` | `string` | - | - | The height of the modal in CSS units. |
| `placement` | `'top' | 'bottom' | 'left' | 'right' | 'center'` | `center` | - | The placement of the modal relative to the viewport. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` | - | - | The icon to display in the modal. |
| `status` | `'info' | 'error' | 'success' | 'warning' | 'question'` | - | - | The status of the modal. |

### DialogHeader

The header of the modal that typically contains the title.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `dismissButton` | `ReactNode` | - | No | Optionally defines a custom dismiss button. |

### DialogContent

The content of the modal.

### DialogFooter

The footer of the modal that typically contains the action buttons.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `hasDivider` | `boolean` | `true` | No | Shows or hides the footer divider. |

### DialogDismissButton

A button that closes the modal.

## Examples

The following examples demonstrate how to use the Modal component in different scenarios.

### Success Modal

The success modal is used to inform the user that an action has been completed successfully.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
import { SuccessIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const SuccessModalExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal message
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog icon={<SuccessIcon />} status="success" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader>Success Modal</DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>This is the message content area.</DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter>
            <Button primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Close
            </Button>
          </DialogFooter>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default SuccessModalExample;
```

### Error Modal

The error modal is used to inform the user that an action has failed.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';

import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const ErrorModalExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal message
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog icon={<ErrorIcon />} status="error" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader>Error Modal</DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>This is the message content area.</DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter>
            <Button primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Close
            </Button>
          </DialogFooter>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default ErrorModalExample;
```

### Warning Modal

The warning modal is used to inform the user that an action may have unintended consequences.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
import { WarningIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const WarningModalExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal message
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog icon={<WarningIcon />} status="warning" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader>Warning Modal</DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>This is the message content area.</DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter>
            <Button primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Continue
            </Button>
            <Button secondary onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Cancel
            </Button>
          </DialogFooter>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default WarningModalExample;
```

### Question Modal

The question modal is used to ask the user for information before proceeding.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
import { QuestionIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato-status';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const QuestionModalExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal message
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog icon={<QuestionIcon />} status="question" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader>Question Modal</DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>This is the message content area.</DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter>
            <Button primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Yes
            </Button>
            <Button secondary onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              No
            </Button>
          </DialogFooter>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default QuestionModalExample;
```

---

This is to focus the user's attention on particular content or isolated action, process or sub-process.

```tsx
import { Button, IconButton } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogDismissButton,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
  DialogSubtitle,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
import { ArrowLeftIcon, CloseIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const ModalPanelExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button id="open-btn" primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal panel
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog aria-labelledby="dialog-label" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)} placement="end">
          <DialogHeader
            id="dialog-label"
            subtitle={<DialogSubtitle>Optional subtitle</DialogSubtitle>}
            leadingButton={
              <IconButton
                id="back-btn"
                aria-label="Back"
                flat
                secondary
                size="small"
                onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}
                icon={<ArrowLeftIcon />}
              />
            }
            dismissButton={
              <DialogDismissButton aria-label="Dismiss dialog" onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
                <CloseIcon />
              </DialogDismissButton>
            }
          >
            Header title
          </DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>
            A modal panel can include a header, a footer and a customizable content area. Modal
            panels typically present information and controls requiring the user to take a specific
            action. The title should be consistent with the action that opened the panel and provide
            context regarding the panel function. Always provide a close or a cancel button. Give
            the action button a label that matches the action.
          </DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter>
            <Button id="ok-btn" primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Action
            </Button>
            <Button id="cancel-btn" secondary onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Cancel
            </Button>
          </DialogFooter>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default ModalPanelExample;
```

## Import

Import `Dialog` to use the component.

```ts
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogHeader,
  DialogSubtitle,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
```

## API Reference

The following exports are available within `@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog`:

### Dialog

The `Dialog` component is used to display a modal dialog.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `size` | `'small' | 'medium' | 'large'` | `medium` | - | The size of the modal. |
| `closeOnBackdropClick` | `boolean` | `true` | - | Whether the modal should close when the backdrop is clicked. |
| `closeOnEscape` | `boolean` | `true` | - | Whether the modal should close when the escape key is pressed. |
| `onClose` | `() => void` | - | - | Function to be called when the modal is closed. |
| `width` | `string` | - | - | The width of the modal in CSS units. |
| `height` | `string` | - | - | The height of the modal in CSS units. |
| `placement` | `'top' | 'bottom' | 'left' | 'right' | 'center'` | `center` | - | The placement of the modal relative to the viewport. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` | - | - | The icon to display in the modal. |
| `status` | `'info' | 'error' | 'success' | 'warning' | 'question'` | - | - | The status of the modal. |

### DialogHeader

The header of the modal that typically contains the title.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `dismissButton` | `ReactNode` | - | No | Optionally defines a custom dismiss button. |
| `leadingButton` | `ReactNode` | - | No | Optionally defines a leading dismiss button. |
| `subtitle` | `ReactNode` | - | No | Optionally defines a subtitle for the modal. |

### DialogContent

The content of the modal.

### DialogFooter

The footer of the modal that typically contains the action buttons.

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |
| -------- | ---- | ------- | -------- | ----------- |
| `hasDivider` | `boolean` | `true` | No | Shows or hides the footer divider. |

### DialogDismissButton

A button that closes the modal.

---

A modal window presents information and controls in a dialog which temporarily prevents the user from doing anything else, and requires the user to take a specific action before proceeding.It retains the user's overall location and context within the application, thus avoiding navigation to other locations, or loading or reloading additional pages in the background.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogFooter,
  DialogHeader,
  DialogSubtitle,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const ModalWindowExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button id="open-btn" primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal window
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog aria-labelledby="dialog-label" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader
            id="dialog-label"
            subtitle={<DialogSubtitle>Optional subtitle</DialogSubtitle>}
          >
            Header title
          </DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>
            A modal window can include a header, a footer and a customizable content area. Modal
            windows typically present information and controls requiring the user to take a specific
            action. The title should be consistent with the action that opened the window and
            provide context regarding the window function. Always provide a close or a cancel
            button. Give the action button a label that matches the action.
          </DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter hasDivider={false}>
            <Button id="ok-btn" primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Action
            </Button>
            <Button id="cancel-btn" secondary onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Cancel
            </Button>
          </DialogFooter>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default ModalWindowExample;
```

## Import

```ts
  DialogSubtitle,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
```

## API Reference

| Property | Type | Default | Required | Description |

| `closeOnEscape` | `boolean` | `true` | - | Whether the modal should close when the escape key is pressed. |
| `onClose` | `() => void` | - | - | Function to be called when the modal is closed. |
| `width` | `string` | - | - | The width of the modal in CSS units. |
| `height` | `string` | - | - | The height of the modal in CSS units. |

## Examples

The following examples demonstrate how to use the Modal window component in different scenarios.

### Modal window without footer

A modal window can also present information to the user without a specific action being required. In this case no footer and action button may be appropriate. A close button can then be shown on the header.

```tsx
import { Button } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/button';
import {
  Dialog,
  DialogContent,
  DialogDismissButton,
  DialogHeader,
} from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/dialog';
import { CloseSmallIcon } from '@ux-aspects-universal/react/icon/jato';
import * as React from 'react';
import { FC, useState } from 'react';

const ModalWindowWithoutFooterExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button id="open-btn" primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal window
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog aria-labelledby="dialog-label" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader
            id="dialog-label"
            dismissButton={
              <DialogDismissButton aria-label="Dismiss dialog" onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
                <CloseSmallIcon />
              </DialogDismissButton>
            }
          >
            Header title
          </DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>
            A modal window can also present information to the user without a specific action being
            required. In this case no footer and action button may be appropriate. A close button
            can then be shown on the header.
          </DialogContent>
        </Dialog>
      )}
    </>
  );
};

export default ModalWindowWithoutFooterExample;
```

### Modal window with content overflow

If the amount of content required on a dialog would cause its height to be excessive then a scrollbar can be used. When the content is oveflowing, gradients appear at the top and bottom of the scrollable area.

```tsx

  DialogHeader,

const ModalWindowOverflowExample: FC = () => {
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);

  return (
    <>
      <Button id="open-btn" primary onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}>
        Show modal window
      </Button>
      {isOpen && (
        <Dialog height={400} aria-labelledby="dialog-label" onClose={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
          <DialogHeader id="dialog-label">Header title</DialogHeader>
          <DialogContent>
            A modal window can include a header, a footer and a customizable content area. Modal
            windows typically present information and controls requiring the user to take a specific
            action. The title should be consistent with the action that opened the window and
            provide context regarding the window function. Always provide a close or a cancel
            button. Give the action button a label that matches the action. A modal window can
            include a header, a footer and a customizable content area. Modal windows typically
            present information and controls requiring the user to take a specific action. The title
            should be consistent with the action that opened the window and provide context
            regarding the window function. Always provide a close or a cancel button. Give the
            action button a label that matches the action. A modal window can include a header, a
            footer and a customizable content area. Modal windows typically present information and
            controls requiring the user to take a specific action. The title should be consistent
            with the action that opened the window and provide context regarding the window
            function. Always provide a close or a cancel button. Give the action button a label that
            matches the action. A modal window can include a header, a footer and a customizable
            content area. Modal windows typically present information and controls requiring the
            user to take a specific action. The title should be consistent with the action that
            opened the window and provide context regarding the window function. Always provide a
            close or a cancel button. Give the action button a label that matches the action. A
            modal window can include a header, a footer and a customizable content area. Modal
            windows typically present information and controls requiring the user to take a specific
            action. The title should be consistent with the action that opened the window and
            provide context regarding the window function. Always provide a close or a cancel
            button. Give the action button a label that matches the action.
          </DialogContent>
          <DialogFooter hasDivider={false}>
            <Button id="ok-btn" primary autoFocus onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>

              Cancel

        </Dialog>
```
